   - N -> C -> B -> A -> S -> F -> T -> B -> G -> J -> Q

   - N -> C -> G -> J -> Q

   - Removing any one of these nodes will not suffice because there are alternate paths.
   - Specifically, removing C interrupts most paths but leaves N -> C -> O -> R -> J -> Q intact.
   - Removing B interrupts many direct paths but leaves N -> C -> O -> R -> J -> Q intact.
   - Removing G or J individually leaves other paths intact.

3. **Minimum cut set:**
   - To disconnect N and Q completely, we need to remove nodes that cover all possible paths.
   - Removing nodes C and J will disrupt all paths between N and Q.

Thus, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 2, and the cut set is [C, J].
